The job row survives with its status and `last_error`, and `deploy_log.php` now says the output was pruned instead of showing an unexplained empty table. It also stopped rendering a `mission_details.php?id=0` link for a system job, which has no mission.
- The `php` container mounts `Ansible/` read-only, outside the webroot. The playbook contract tests used to skip there because the directory was not mounted, which is how a skipped test silently stops guarding anything; they now read the file the deploy would actually upload, resolved through the same `ansible_source_dir()` the worker uses. In the same pass `lib/ansible.php` shed the inventory transport and its parsers into `lib/ansible_inventory.php` (1172 → 845 lines): one file was carrying deploy-artifact generation and the read-only inventory pull, two unrelated jobs (ADR-0006, split by domain).
- The audit log grew a security channel and now records what was previously invisible (categories are a `VARCHAR`, so no migration). **New `auth` category:** successful and failed sign-ins (failures name the typed user name, bounded and escaped on render), the lockout transitions themselves (account after 5 failures, IP rate limit), sign-ins refused for locked or deactivated accounts (the HTTP response stays the generic "invalid"; only the log learns the difference, consistent with the anti-enumeration handling), sign-outs, server-side session expiry (logged once, before `logout()` wipes the session the entry is attributed to), and the self-service password change incl. its wrong-current-password failure — closing the gap where `deploy_login_attempts` was written but displayed nowhere. The channel is flood-bounded: rejected attempts while the IP rate limit is active are counted but not audited, and the onset itself is one line (`auth_record_failed_login()`), so an unauthenticated client gets at most the below-limit failures plus one row per window into `deploy_logs` instead of one row per request. That table also had no retention and grew without bound; it is a 15-minute lockout counter, so the maintenance worker now prunes it on the shared 7-day window (`repo_purge_login_attempts`). **Refusals are no longer silent:** all portal permission gates (14) and CSRF gates (15) now go through `portal_forbid()` / `portal_reject_csrf()` (new `lib/audit_events.php`, loaded via bootstrap), which audit the denial (permission or page, acting user if any) and keep the response byte-identical (403/400 + localized plain text). The stale-token case on the login form and the no-session logout stay quiet by design. **Data egress is recorded:** the full-mission JSON export (allowed for any signed-in user, so worth a trace) and both CSV list exports with row counts. **Deploy jobs log their outcome**, not just their queueing: `deploy_worker_audit_outcome()` writes succeeded/failed (+ bounded error) per mission deploy, attributed to the operator who queued it; inventory system jobs are deliberately excluded (an interval "succeeded" per credential every few hours would bury real events; their one durable consequence, the ESXi auth pause, is audited at its two transitions instead: onset in the worker, resume on credential save). **Updates now name their changes:** `audit_change_summary()` diffs the stored row against what was actually persisted — VM updates reload the saved row, so a validator normalization (an emptied hostname falling back to the VM name, an empty guest id to the default) is never logged as a change that did not happen (trimmed-string compare, so NULL equals ''), renders `field: "old" -> "new"` with per-value and per-summary caps that report their own truncation, and withholds opaque fields (mission/VM notes as "changed"; a credential secret rotation as "secret: changed", the value never read back). Applied to mission, VM and credential updates; a no-op save logs "(no field changes)", which under optimistic locking is a real event. **The logs page went from three tabs to four** so the landing tab answers the security question: Sicherheit (auth, users, credentials), Ressourcen (missions, VMs, VLANs, OS), Deployment (deploy, mecm), System & Integrationen (system, settings, legacy API); the old `access` tab key had no deep links anywhere, so the rename breaks nothing. New `LogTaxonomyTest` (every category in exactly one tab, security is the default tab, every category/tab has a translated label), `AuditEventsTest` (diff edges: opaque fields never leak, empty renders as `(empty)`, newlines collapse, truncation admits itself) and `AuthAuditTest` (integration: login success/failure/lockout audited, empty submits are not events). DE/EN keys in `logs` and `help` updated (four-tab description, a new paragraph on what the security tab holds, corrected settings/user-management cross-references).
- The confirm dialog's action buttons are centred, matching the session-expiry modal. `.modal-confirm` carried a `justify-content: flex-end` override in `components.css` that pushed its buttons right while icon and title stayed centred; the override is removed, so both modals share the centred `.modal-actions` base rule. Button order (dismiss left, accept right) and the left-aligned message text are unchanged; ADR-0013 now records the centred action row as the baseline for any future dialog. Playwright-verified on both modals.
- **`generate_token` silently invalidated a working MECM token.** The button was always rendered, the handler overwrote the stored hash unconditionally, and nothing asked: one click and the token in the MECM server's registry stopped being accepted, with heartbeats failing until somebody noticed. It now asks whenever a token already exists, and reads "Token neu generieren" in that state instead of pretending it creates the first one. Generating the first token stays promptless, since nothing is lost. `clear_token` became `.button-danger`, correcting an earlier call of mine: "you can generate a new one" is replacement, not undo, and the secret is unrecoverable. Help text `settings_token_p3` quoted button labels that no longer exist and now matches the UI.
- The confirmation guard was inverted from guessing to classifying, because guessing is what let `generate_token` through. It used to match destructive verbs in the action name, so `generate_token`, `set_role` and `set_active` were invisible to it. Now every action a portal form can POST (39 today) is either confirmed in markup or listed in `SAFE_ACTIONS` with the reason it cannot lose anything, and the test also rejects stale or self-contradicting entries. A new action fails the build until somebody decides which it is. Verified by breaking it three ways: dropping the `generate_token` prompt, adding a fresh unconfirmed `rebuild_cache` action, and leaving a stale `SAFE_ACTIONS` key. `deploy.php:start` and `users.php:clear_lock` are the two entries whose safety is a judgement, and both now carry that judgement in writing.
- `set_role` and `set_active` gained prompts, and the audit behind them corrected the help. The server already refuses to deactivate your own account or to strip the last active admin, but it permits an admin to demote *themselves* while another admin exists: that closes user management to them immediately, and only the other admin can undo it. `usersmgmt_safety_4` now says so, where the old list implied self-lockout was impossible. Deactivation asks only on the destructive branch of the toggle, never before activating, and both prompts name the account (`:name`) so a row action cannot be confirmed against the wrong row.
- Two smaller edges. A conditional prompt must **omit** `data-confirm`, not render it empty, because `[data-confirm]` matches a blank value; app.js now treats blank as "not configured" and lets the submit through instead of opening a dialog with no question in it, and the guard test rejects a blank value outright. And `__t()` substitutes placeholders but does not pluralize, so `os.confirm_delete` read "wird von 0 VM(s) genutzt" for an unused operating system; the count now picks the sentence (`_unused` / `_one` / `_many`), and a mission with no VMs stopped claiming it would delete "alle zugehörigen VMs".
- The credential connection test stopped pasting PHP internals into the portal. `credential_test_esxi()` / `credential_test_ssh()` built their flash by concatenating `$exception->getMessage()`, and because `virtusphere_install_error_handlers()` promotes every warning to an `ErrorException`, a bad hostname rendered as `ESXi API request failed: file_get_contents(): php_network_getaddresses: getaddrinfo for assd failed: No address associated with hostname` — untranslated, twice on the page, and exactly what `portal.md` forbids. The test functions now return `['ok', 'code', 'detail', 'context']`: `code` is a `VIRTUSPHERE_INVENTORY_ERROR_*` category, `detail` is the redacted raw text. The portal maps the category to a localized sentence and renders the detail behind a native `<details>` in the alert; the same text also goes to `error.log` and the audit row records the category. Rather than invent a second vocabulary, the existing fetch-error categories (the SSoT behind `deploy_esxi_inventory_state.last_error_category`) gained `dns`, `tls`, `http` and `config`, and `integrations.php` stopped printing that column's raw English (`unreachable`) at the user. Two classifiers feed the one list on purpose: `ansible_categorize_inventory_error()` reads playbook stdout, the new `connection_error_category()` reads PHP/OpenSSL/phpseclib wording. **Three defects surfaced on the way.** `credential_http_status()` returned the *first* status line of `$http_response_header`, so a followed redirect reported its `302` instead of the endpoint's real answer. `flash_set()` appended without de-duplication, which is why a double-click on "Testen" stacked two nearly identical alerts; it now drops exact repeats and caps the queue. And the detail is redacted (`Authorization: Basic`, the secret itself) before it reaches session, log or HTML, skipping secrets under four characters because `str_replace()` would shred the message. New `ConnectionErrorTest` (9 tests) plus a `PhaseCContractTest` guard, because a page that echoes the removed `'message'` key is not a typo but a fatal on every connection test — which is precisely what a stray revert of `portal/credentials.php` produced mid-change, caught by driving the real portal rather than by any test then present.
- **Two mismatches between the connection test and the product are now documented, not fixed.** Every ESXi playbook sets `validate_certs: false` and talks pyVmomi/SOAP to `/sdk`, while `credential_test_esxi()` passes no `ssl` context options and therefore verifies the peer certificate, as PHP has done by default since 5.6. A stock ESXi with its self-signed host certificate makes "Testen" report `tls` while the inventory pull works. Separately, the test probes `/rest/appliance/system/version`, which is the vCenter appliance API; a standalone ESXi answers 404, now visible as `http` instead of hidden in a generic failure. Both are false negatives on correct credentials, both are a security or protocol decision rather than a bug fix, and both are written up in `docs/operations/esxi-inventory.md` with a pointer at the call site so nobody flips `verify_peer` in passing.
- Every transaction in `Docker/WebAPI/lib` now opens through the re-entrant `repo_transaction()`. The thirteen remaining raw `begin_transaction()` blocks (deploy-job create/group/claim/cancel/reap/append-log, template clone and capture, `repo_save_vm`, the legacy `vmListTo*` batches, `repo_reset_vm_mecm_id`, mission import, VLAN reassign) were migrated with unchanged semantics; the legacy batches keep swallowing failures into `0`/`false` for the desktop client. Motive: MySQL answers a nested `BEGIN` by silently committing the outer transaction, and `repo_transaction()`'s depth tracker cannot see a raw `BEGIN`, so the two idioms side by side were a landmine for every future composition — now any repo function may safely call any other. The two legacy machine-API scripts (`db_importMAC.php`, `mecm_packages.php`) keep their single outermost request transaction as the documented exception (`GROK.md` forbidden pattern, `webapi.md`); nothing nests them, and they call no `repo_transaction()`-wrapped repo. Also in this hardening pass: the deploy page's storage table loads the datastore cache in one grouped query instead of one per ESXi credential (`repo_esxi_inventory_datastore_rows()`); an invalid stored portal timezone now renders visibly under "Aktuell eingestellt" instead of the browser silently marking Europe/Berlin as selected and the next save overwriting the misconfiguration (saving the invalid value back fails validation with a message); `lang/de/login.php` lost its last ASCII transliterations (`vorübergehend`, `ungültig`); and `LangCatalogTest` asserts DE/EN module-list parity first, so a container bind-mount glob hiccup (seen once, unreproducible) names the missing module instead of dumping a thousand-key diff.
- `repo_esxi_inventory_replace_kind()` is now atomic by itself. It rewrites a credential's rows as DELETE followed by INSERTs and was safe only because every production caller happened to wrap it — `repo_esxi_inventory_apply()` did, direct callers did not, and its own docblock already promised atomicity. A committed empty middle is not a cosmetic race: `repo_esxi_vlan_sync()` reads that table as positive evidence and would retire the host's portgroups, un-retiring them on the next pull. The new re-entrant `repo_transaction()` helper makes the guarantee local (MySQL has no nested transactions and answers a second `BEGIN` by committing the outer one, so nesting joins rather than opens). The sync also read `$present` and the fetch-state *before* opening its own transaction, deciding against a snapshot a concurrent rewrite could already have replaced; both reads moved inside. Finally, "fresh successful fetch" meant `last_status = 'ok'` alone, and a state row can carry that word with no `last_success_at` at all (backfill, hand-edited row — one existed in the dev database for a credential whose host does not even resolve); the guard now demands the timestamp, which is what `docs/operations/esxi-inventory.md` always claimed. Both new fixtures were checked against the un-fixed code and do fail there.

- The domain fields had silently stopped validating in the browser. An `<input pattern>` is compiled as an ECMAScript regex with the `v` flag, not as PCRE, and under `v` a literal hyphen inside a character class is a reserved syntax character. `[A-Za-z0-9-]` therefore threw `Invalid character class`, Chromium discarded the whole pattern, and `mission_details.php` / `vm_edit.php` accepted anything into the domain field without an inline complaint. Nothing caught it: `php -l`, `node --check`, the lang audit and the unit suite never compile the attribute, and `Validator::fqdn()` still rejected the value on submit, so the only symptom was a console error nobody reads. Escaping the hyphen (`\-`) restores the client-side check with byte-identical accept/reject behaviour, because PCRE reads `\-` as the same literal. The regex was also copied across both pages; it now lives once as `VIRTUSPHERE_DNS_LABEL_PATTERN` / `VIRTUSPHERE_FQDN_INPUT_PATTERN` in `lib/validate.php`, beside the `fqdn()` rule it mirrors, and `fqdn()` consumes the same constant. New `DomainInputPatternTest` pins all three halves: no `pattern` attribute in any portal page carries an unescaped hyphen in a character class, the two domain inputs echo the constant instead of a literal copy, and the HTML pattern and the server validator agree on twelve sample domains. Browser-verified: `validity.patternMismatch` is true again for `-bad.example.com`, `bad-.example.com`, `nodot` and `a_b.example.com`, false for `corp.example.local` and `ab-cd.ef-gh.local`, console clean.
- Confirmation prompts moved from `window.confirm()` to a shared portal dialog (ADR-0013). The `data-confirm` attribute stays the only markup contract, so all thirteen existing prompts (missions, templates, VMs and the VM bulk actions, VLANs, operating systems, credentials, deploy jobs and job groups, VLAN reassign, MECM resets) upgraded without touching their call sites. The dialog is a native `<dialog>` opened with `showModal()`, rendered once in `layout_footer()`, and the session-expiry warning was migrated onto the same `.modal` base so the portal has one modal component instead of two. The accept button names its action ("Löschen") instead of saying OK, takes the danger variant from a `.button-danger` trigger, and can be overridden with `data-confirm-action` where the trigger's label would collide with the dialog's own "Abbrechen" (deploy jobs). **The replay path is the subtle part:** `window.confirm()` blocked, so the click continued natively and the browser carried the button along as the form's *submitter*; a dialog cannot block, so the click is cancelled and replayed with `form.requestSubmit(trigger)`. A plain `form.submit()` would have dropped that submitter and silently stripped `name="action"`, turning delete into a no-op on `os.php`, `credentials.php` and the VM bulk actions. Constraint validation now runs before the dialog opens, so an empty required field no longer costs an answered prompt. Escape, backdrop click and dismiss all cancel, focus returns to the trigger, and the page behind scroll-locks. Without JavaScript the POST goes through unchanged: the confirmation was always UX, never the gate.
- A portal-wide audit of destructive actions closed four spots that had no confirmation at all: deleting a machine-API IP allowlist entry (which also rendered as a plain button rather than a destructive one, and cuts off MECM/Ansible clients from that address), clearing the report token, and resetting another user's password. Unlocking a locked-out account stays unconfirmed on purpose, since it is a reversible remediation rather than a destructive act. Deleting a **template** also stopped claiming it would delete "this mission and all related VMs" and now gets its own wording.
- That contract is now enforced rather than reviewed. `tests/Static/PortalConfirmContractTest.php` (8 tests, in the normal `unit` suite) fails when a destructive form action or a `.button-danger` submit ships without `data-confirm`, when a prompt is a literal instead of a `__t()` key, when a page hand-rolls `window.confirm()`/`alert()` or a second dialog, and when the accepted click is replayed with `form.submit()`. Every guard was run against a deliberately broken tree, so each is known to *fail*, not merely to pass; the `form.submit()` guard is regex-scoped so the legitimate `logoutForm.submit()` in the session timer does not trip it, and comments are stripped before scanning because they name the very patterns they forbid. A legitimately unconfirmed action is exempted in `UNCONFIRMED_BY_DESIGN` with its reason (today only `clear_lock`). The `window.confirm()` fallback for browsers without `<dialog>` was dropped in the same step: `components.css` already relies on `:has()`, which no engine shipped before `<dialog>`, so the fallback protected no real browser while contradicting the rule. Such a browser now behaves exactly like one with JavaScript disabled, which the ADR already sanctions.
- Both modals moved out of `lib/layout.php` into `lib/layout_modals.php` (`layout_confirm_dialog()`, `layout_session_modal()`), split by domain per ADR-0006. The session modal takes the hidden logout form and the countdown JSON island with it, since nothing else uses them. **Extraction created a new silent failure mode and the test now covers it:** a `layout_footer()` that stops calling `layout_confirm_dialog()` ships a portal where every `data-confirm` button submits with no prompt, no dialog and no error, so `PortalConfirmContractTest` pins the `require_once` and both calls inside `layout_footer()` itself, and was checked against a footer with the call deleted. The test also now rejects a `<dialog>` in any portal page, not just a second confirm dialog. Honest bookkeeping: this did not clear the 400-line soft warning it was meant to address. `layout.php` went from 480 to 452 lines, still above ADR-0006's threshold; the remaining bulk is `layout_header()`'s chrome markup plus the connection-error message mapping, and the next split by domain would move `VIRTUSPHERE_CONNECTION_MESSAGE_KEYS`/`connection_error_message()` into the `lib/connection_errors.php` it is already named after.
- The German portal calls the vSphere objects by their English names: **Datacenter** replaces **Rechenzentrum** everywhere (`Datastore` already read that way). "Rechenzentrum" is the physical building, while the thing the inventory pull reads is the logical container object of the vSphere inventory — a standalone host's is literally named `ha-datacenter`, which the help text already quoted next to the word it contradicted. VMware's own German client avoids the translation too and says "Datencenter"; the customer's admins use the English terms, so those ship untranslated rather than as "Datencenter"/"Datenspeicher". Values only: the twenty-one strings live in `lang/de/{integrations,help,deploy,mission_details,vm_edit,missions,settings,validate}.php`, no key moved, so DE/EN parity was never at risk, and `docs/operations/esxi-inventory.md` followed. German plural is endingless (`die Datacenter`), so `mission_details.datacenter_optional_hint` was rewritten rather than substituted: "Bei mehreren Rechenzentren (vCenter)" would have forced the dative "Datacentern". Grammar aside, `dev_field_vm_datacenter`/`vm_datastore` said "(VM-Abweichung)" where English said "(VM override)" — inside a table headed "Abweichungen" every row is a deviation, so the parenthetical said nothing and implied the mission rows above were not; both now read "(VM-Override)", the word the migration `0014_normalize_vm_location_overrides` and the operations doc already use. Verified headless across seven portal pages, scanning `textContent` (which covers the JS-hidden tab panels) plus the `title`/`placeholder`/`aria-label` attributes that carry the missions-list deviation tooltip.
- `EsxiVlanSyncTest` no longer depends on an empty database. `repo_esxi_vlan_sync()` reads the *entire* portgroup cache and the *entire* fetch-state table by design, not one credential's slice, so a dev database holding a real ESXi credential decided two of the fixtures instead of the fixture doing it: its one cached portgroup made `testEmptyResultKeepsCatalog`'s "empty result" set non-empty, which armed the retire branch and retired the very VLAN the empty-guard exists to protect, and any successful pull of its own would have armed `testNoRetireWithoutFreshFetch` the same way. CI stayed green because its database has neither. `setUp` now parks the foreign portgroup rows and fetch-state rows and `tearDown` restores them, extending the snapshot/restore dance the class already performed for `deploy_vlan`. No production code changed; the guard was always right.

## 2026-07-09

- Datacenter and datastore became plain dropdowns, and the deploy page now says what a rollout will cost in disk space (ADR-0023 amendment). The compound control (`inventory_combo_field()`: a free-text `<input>` plus a convenience `<select>`) made the two most consequential mission fields look free-form and needed two paragraphs of hint text to explain itself. It is replaced by one shared hard select (`inventory_select_field()`) built on the same stored-unknown fallback the VLAN selects already used: a value the inventory does not know renders as an extra selected option marked "nicht im aktuellen Inventar" and is never dropped, matched exactly so a case variant shows the suffix instead of being silently re-cased on the next save (the warn logic stays case-insensitive and never fires on it). **The lockout guard that justified permanent free text became a server-side branch:** with zero cached names of a kind the field is a plain input, so an empty cache still cannot lock anyone out; once names exist, new ones arrive through an inventory pull, as they always did. Accepted trade-off, now documented: a value living only on a never-pulled credential must wait for that credential's first pull. No wire change — the machine API, mission import and `repo_validate_mission_values()` keep accepting any string. **The mission datacenter now hides adaptively** under the rule the per-VM override already used (empty value, exact options, exactly one name), posting a hidden input so saving cannot wipe it, and reading the `form_old()` value so a validation failure with a filled datacenter shows the field again; with a single standalone host the field and its long hint disappear entirely. Datastore options carry the free space of the last pull as a label (the value stays the bare name): per credential in grouped mode, the **minimum** across credentials in the flat union, because the target host is not chosen yet and only the tightest number is safe (`esxi_inventory_free_union()`, `free_bytes` was already cached since migration 0013 — no DDL). The VM editor's inherit option names the mission value (`Von Mission übernehmen (ds01)`), replacing the input placeholder a `<select>` cannot have; nothing is prefilled, so the migration-0014 defect stays fixed. **New: a storage requirement table on the deploy page** (`lib/deploy_storage.php`). `ansible_storage_by_datastore()` sums the *provisioned* disk sizes of the selected VMs per target datastore, reusing `ansible_vm_disks()` and `ansible_effective_datastore()` — including the default disk a VM without rows gets — so the estimate and what `createVMs-ESXi_playbook.yml` provisions cannot drift apart; ten VMs at 50 GB read as 500 GB, a VM with fifteen mixed disks as their sum, and a per-VM override gets its own row. The requirement is compared against the chosen credential's cached `free_bytes` and marked sufficient / probably too small / unknown. It is **warn-only and never a gate**, consistent with the cache-never-blocks rule: no credential, no cached datastore or a NULL free value all render as "keine Angabe", not a refusal. The schedule preview renders the verdict server-side (credential and VM selection are known there), so it holds without JavaScript; the queue form renders the requirement and a new CSP-nonced JSON island lets `initDeployStorage()` refill free space, verdict and the projected capacity bar on every credential change and VM (de)selection, mirroring the server's "no checkbox checked = whole mission" rule. `backup_status_human_bytes()`'s body moved into a portal-wide `virtusphere_human_bytes()` (`lib/format.php`) that PHP and JS now share; the JS mirror is pinned by the same dash/unit table. `data-combo-clear` disappeared from markup and `app.js` together (only the RAM presets still use the compound field), `.inventory-field` CSS dropped, and `initCapacityBars()` grew a shared `applyCapacityFill()`. New `EsxiInventoryFreeUnionTest` (min wins, NULL ignored, case keys merge) and `AnsibleStorageEstimateTest` (GB→bytes, thin counts provisioned, default-disk fallback, override rows, case/padding merge, template's empty key); `PortalComboHooksTest` gained the `data-storage-*` markup↔JS contract. DE/EN keys across `common` (`free_suffix`), `mission_details`, `vm_edit`, `deploy` and `help` (new "Speicherbedarf beim Einreihen" section); `datastore_pick`, `datacenter_pick` and `location_custom` dropped. Docs: ADR-0023 amendment, `docs/operations/esxi-inventory.md`.
- Creator became a server-owned provenance field, and missions gained one (migration `0015_mission_creator`: `deploy_missions.mission_creator VARCHAR(255) NULL`, a username snapshot like `vm_creator`, not an FK, so it survives a user being renamed or deleted). The VM editor's `Ersteller` was a plain text input whose value was read back from `$_POST` and rewritten on every save, validated only as an optional string, so any writer could set the creator to an arbitrary value or blank it; `readonly` alone would not have helped, because readonly inputs still submit. Ownership now lives at the repository choke point instead of in the page: `repo_save_vm()` stamps `vm_creator` from the acting user on create and re-reads the stored value on update, `repo_create_mission()` does the same for `mission_creator`, and both ignore whatever the payload claims, so a new caller can neither forget nor forge it. The portal renders both fields `readonly` and without a `name` attribute. `repo_validate_vm_payload()` still accepts `vm_creator` deliberately: mission import (`mission_transfer.php`) and the legacy desktop API (`vmListToCreate`/`vmListToUpdate`) carry their own, and only those paths do. Template semantics follow the work rather than the file: capturing a mission into a template keeps each VM's original author but attributes the template row to whoever captured it, while instantiating a template re-stamps the new mission *and* its cloned VMs to the operator (`repo_clone_mission_vms()` gained an explicit `$creator` parameter rather than switching on its `$reason` string). An imported mission belongs to the importer; `mission_creator` is not part of the transfer format and is never read from the untrusted file, while exported VMs keep their `vm_creator`. Missions created through the legacy token API stay empty rather than being attributed to someone: `access.php` resolves only the token's *role*, not a user id, and extending that surface is out of scope. Pre-migration rows are not backfilled, because the audit log records the author by mission *name* and renames break that link; an unknown creator renders as a muted `Unbekannt` placeholder on an empty field, replacing the old `?? $user['name']` fallback that displayed the current user as the author and would have persisted that name on the next save. **Wire note (at introduction):** `mecm-api.php` embedded the mission row via `SELECT *`, so `getDeviceInfos`/`getDeviceList` gained an additive `mission.mission_creator` key; PowerShell consumers ignored unknown properties. ADR-0019/E3 later narrowed `getDeviceInfos`, while `getDeviceList` keeps the mission object. Verified against the running stack: a forged `vm_creator` posted from the DOM and one passed straight into `repo_save_vm()` both leave the column untouched, create stamps the acting user, and the capture, clone, import and legacy paths each produce the authorship described above (new forbidden pattern in `GROK.md`).
- Hot-Add (CPU/RAM) in the VM editor lines up with the form grid and reads as a real option set. `.checkbox-grid` grew an `.checkbox-grid-aligned` variant that mirrors the `.form-grid` tracks (and its breakpoints), so a fixed option pair is exactly as wide as the fields above it, while open-ended lists (packages, deploy's mission VMs) keep the `auto-fit` stretch. `.checkbox-item` gained hover, checked (accent border + ~8 % accent wash, no solid fill) and disabled states; the keyboard focus ring is *relocated* onto the card via `:has(input:focus-visible)` using the same 2px accent outline as the global `:focus-visible` rule. A hand-rolled translucent halo was tried first and dropped: it silently lost to the `:has(input:checked)` rule on specificity, which left keyboard focus with no visible indicator at all, and even once fixed it measured 1.8:1 against the page background instead of the required 3:1. Measured contrast of the shipped ring is 7.57:1 (dark) and 3.59:1 (light). `.field-label` is now styled like the field captions it sits beside, and `.hint` prose is muted, 13 px and capped at ~78ch inside a form section (ADR-0013 updated).

- Help-page render fix, backup-explainer dedupe and a portal favicon. **Fix:** `portal/help.php` never required `lib/deploy_constants.php`, so the ESXi traffic-light thresholds the integrations help panel interpolates (introduced by the multi-host hardening below) were undefined constants at render time: PHP 8 threw mid-page and the response was silently truncated after the integrations panel (still HTTP 200, nothing in the logs, the tab bar renders before the crash), leaving the Architecture, Roles & Users and Settings help tabs completely empty. One `require_once` in `help.php` fixes it; all eight tabs Playwright-verified in light and dark afterwards. Worth remembering: `php -l`, lang-audit and the unit suite cannot catch an undefined constant that only an include path at render time resolves. **Dedupe:** the backup card's collapsible explainer on the settings page repeated the help Architecture tab nearly verbatim (`settings.backup_ops_*` vs `help.ops_jobs_*`/`ops_restore_*`); the card now keeps only the actionable part — the `install-backup-schedule.sh` one-liner — plus a deep link to `help.php#panel-stack`, and the one settings-only fact (dump min-size/gzip check, pruning) moved into `help.ops_jobs_backup`. Five `settings` keys dropped, `settings.backup_ops_more` added (DE/EN); `docs/operations/backup.md` card description updated. **Favicon:** the portal never declared one, so every page logged a console 404 for `/favicon.ico`; both heads (`layout.php` and the standalone `login.php`) now link the existing `assets/img/logo-64.png` through `layout_asset_url()`.
- Multi-host hardening of the ESXi inventory / VLAN catalog layer (ADR-0023 amendment), from an audit against fleets of standalone hosts (many same-named portgroups). **Fixes:** "Refresh all" no longer bypasses the auth pause — it skips paused credentials and breaks the outcome down in the flash (queued / already pending / paused skipped / failed, plus a settings hint when no unambiguous Ansible credential exists); only the targeted per-credential refresh stays a deliberate retry (`esxi_inventory_refresh_all_targets`). The traffic light no longer cries "stale" with interval 0 (automation deliberately off; only real failures colour it), its thresholds became constants (`VIRTUSPHERE_ESXI_INVENTORY_FAILURE_STREAK_DANGER`, `VIRTUSPHERE_ESXI_INVENTORY_STALE_FACTOR`) interpolated into a new on-page legend and the help, and the "Stand" timestamp now reads the fetch-state row (SSoT) instead of a coalesce chain over arbitrary inventory rows. **Visibility:** the VLAN catalog answers presence per column — "on X of Y hosts" with the missing hosts named (Y counts only credentials with a successful pull), and a new VLAN-ID column that flags same-name-different-ID across hosts as a danger badge (the previously invisible wrong-network failure class); the inventory playbook now forwards raw portgroup objects and the unit-testable PHP parser extracts name + integer `vlan_id` (trunk ranges flagged, never compared; `meta_json`, no DDL; wrong field paths degrade to silence via `default()` + empty-guard, verified against the productive host on rollout). The deploy page gained a live per-host warning (CSP-nonced JSON island + `data-deploy-esxi` hook): values present on other hosts but missing from the chosen one, disjoint by construction from the mission-level union warning (union misses are subtracted, no value ever appears twice). The deviation report now includes templates, flagged, so a stale VLAN living only in a template can no longer hide (and unlocks the reassign form); mission-list badge and deploy hint keep excluding templates. Deviation VLAN values link to the reassign form and prefill "Von" (kills the free-text typo risk). **Correctness/queue:** the catalog upsert follows the reported name case with a deterministic winner (lowest credential id; the old `SELECT DISTINCT` over the `*_ci` column picked one nondeterministically, which would have made the case flap between syncs), using the MySQL 8.4 row-alias upsert; mission deploys claim before mission-less system jobs (`ORDER BY (mission_id IS NULL), id`) so an interval burst of 8 inventory pulls cannot delay an operator's deploy (documented starvation trade-off). **SSoT cleanups:** one name-normalization helper `esxi_inventory_name_key()` replaces six hand-rolled `mb_strtolower(trim())` sites; `repo_active_vlans()` replaces three copied retired-filters; a shared `vlan_select_field()` replaces the two hand-rolled VLAN selects with the stored-unknown fallback option (mission editor + VM interface rows). New tests: `EsxiInventoryAmpelTest`, `EsxiInventoryMissingValuesTest`, `EsxiVlanIdAggregateTest`, `DeployClaimPriorityTest`, plus extensions in `EsxiInventoryEnqueueTest`, `EsxiInventoryCacheTest`, `EsxiVlanSyncTest` (case refresh + determinism), `EsxiVlanReassignTest` (template flag) and `PortalComboHooksTest`/`AnsibleInventoryParseTest` (new markup/parse contracts). DE/EN keys across `integrations`, `vlans`, `deploy`, `settings`-help and `help`; docs: ADR-0023 amendment (incl. the visibility conventions and the deliberate select-vs-report case exception), `docs/operations/esxi-inventory.md` (refresh-pause behaviour, constant-based ampel legend, partial presence + VLAN-ID columns, one-time field verification).
- The backup card names its schedule and its next run instead of hiding both (ADR-0024). Cron, unlike a systemd timer, has no "when do you next fire" interface, so the two tempting fixes were both rejected: a second copy of the schedule (a portal setting or PHP constant) disagrees with the real crontab the first time someone edits one and not the other, and "last run + 24 h" is a guess presented as a fact that breaks for any non-daily schedule and moves whenever someone runs a backup by hand. Instead **the scheduler reports its own schedule**. `scripts/backup.sh` resolves, on every run, what started it (explicit `VIRTUSPHERE_BACKUP_SCHEDULE` → a systemd timer's exact `NextElapseUSecRealtime` → the cron file it actually runs from, honouring `CRON_TZ` → the invoking user's crontab) and appends `schedule`, `schedule_tz`, `schedule_source` and `next_ts` to the existing JSONL status line; detection is best-effort and can never fail a backup. New `lib/cron_schedule.php` is a dependency-free five-field evaluator (`*`, steps, ranges, lists, month/weekday names, `7` as Sunday, the `@daily` family, cron's day-of-month/day-of-week OR rule) that returns **null** for everything it does not fully understand (`@reboot`, seconds fields, `L`, `?`, `#`, out-of-range values) rather than a wrong date. `lib/backup_status.php` derives the expected run with the same precedence and only falls back to the interval when nothing was reported; staleness became healthchecks.io semantics — overdue at *expected run + grace*, not at a fixed age — so `VIRTUSPHERE_BACKUP_STALE_AFTER_SECONDS` is now derived from the new `VIRTUSPHERE_BACKUP_INTERVAL_SECONDS` + `VIRTUSPHERE_BACKUP_GRACE_SECONDS` instead of being an independent magic 26, and a **weekly** backup is no longer permanently "stale". Provenance is shown, not hidden: the card prints the expression and the file it was read from, labels an interval fallback as an estimate, and shows *no* next run at all when a missed run's successor cannot be derived. New `scripts/install-backup-schedule.sh` writes `/etc/cron.d/virtusphere-backup` (`--schedule`, `--user`, `--show`, `--remove`), which is the same file `backup.sh` reads back, so there is no second source to drift from. Status lines written before this change lack the fields and degrade to the estimate; no migration or backfill. The card gained Zeitplan / Nächster Lauf / Überfällig-ab rows plus a collapsible section answering where the cron entry lives, what the run does, where the dumps are, how to restore, and that the mission JSON export and list CSV export are **transfer paths, not backups** — the question that triggered this change. The help panel's "Betrieb im Alltag" now states outright that the backup is the *only* cron job and that deploy-worker (5 s) and maintenance-worker (15 s, MECM probe every 5 min, ESXi pull on its configured interval) are loop containers with no schedule. ADR-0021's security boundary is untouched: the schedule rides the same read-only metadata channel, and there is still no dump download and no web restore. New `CronScheduleNextTest` (23 cases incl. DST fall-back, weekday OR rule, and every unsupported syntax) and 8 new cases in `BackupStatusReadTest`; DE/EN keys across `settings`, `help` and `dashboard` (the stale banner no longer hardcodes "26 hours"); `docs/operations/backup.md` and ADR-0024 (+index).
- The mission datacenter became optional and is resolved at deploy time (ADR-0023). It is only needed when the job runs, and by then the target host is known, so requiring it on every mission stored a copy of a derivable value: exactly the defect migration 0014 had just removed one level below. The resolution chain is now **VM override, then mission, then the sole datacenter of the chosen ESXi credential** (`ansible_effective_datacenter($mission, $vm, $hostDatacenter)`). The third level only ever fires when that credential reports **exactly one** datacenter, i.e. a standalone ESXi's implicit `ha-datacenter`; zero (never pulled) or several (vCenter) is not a guess the portal makes. **No live ESXi read happens at deploy time** — an inventory pull is a queued job (SSH preflight, SFTP upload, remote `ansible-playbook`), and making a deploy wait on one would invert the ADR-0023 guarantee that the cache never blocks a deploy; the resolution reads the cache of the chosen credential instead (`repo_esxi_sole_datacenter()`, in the repo layer so `repo/deploy_jobs.php` can use it without a require cycle). `repo_deploy_assert_mission_ready()` gained an optional `$esxiCredentialId` (default 0 keeps the strict old behaviour) and both enqueue paths pass it; `ansible_assert_mission_ready()` re-checks at run time because the cache can change between queueing and running, and fails the job loudly rather than deploying into a guessed location. `portal/deploy.php` performs the same check *before* the branch, because the schedule-preview path reaches no gate at all, and turns it into a localized `ValidationException` naming the reason (no inventory vs. several candidates, which are listed) — the repo exceptions stay English operator diagnostics. The **datastore stays mandatory** everywhere: a host almost always has several and `vmware_guest` has no default for it, while `datacenter` defaults to `ha-datacenter`. Consequently the mission editor no longer preselects a lone datacenter (it still preselects a lone datastore), its field lost `required`, and its empty picker option now reads "Vom Zielhost übernehmen". Existing mission values are **not** auto-cleared, unlike the VM copies: a mission datacenter can be a deliberate choice and with several credentials its redundancy cannot be proven. No migration, no DDL — `hypervisor_datacenter` was already nullable. Import, template clone and the legacy token-API `updateMission` already ran with `requireLocation = false` and are unchanged; no machine-API wire field moved. New `DeployDatacenterResolutionTest`, five new cases in `AnsibleLocationOverrideTest`, and `MissionsRepoTest` now pins the new contract (datastore required, datacenter optional).
- Two defects in the change below, found by a headless-browser pass and fixed before it shipped. (1) **The RAM preset picker was dead**: `app.js` had been generalized from `data-ram-*` to `data-combo-*` while the markup in `vm_edit.php` kept the old attribute names, so choosing a preset filled nothing. `php -l`, `node --check`, the unit suite and the CSP lint all passed — the contract between markup and JS is pure text. Fixed, plus a new `PortalComboHooksTest` that pins it (no `data-ram-` may remain; every file with `data-combo-picker` needs a `data-combo-input`; `app.js` must read every combo attribute the markup emits). (2) **An empty inventory accused stored values**: `inventory_combo_field()` labelled any stored value "(nicht im aktuellen Inventar)" when the inventory of that kind was empty, contradicting both the deviation report and ADR-0023's rule that an empty cache is no absence proof. The picker and the report now share one predicate (`esxi_inventory_value_unknown()`, fed by a new `name_set` on `esxi_inventory_options()`), so they can never disagree again.
- Per-VM datacenter/datastore overrides now actually reach the deploy, and both fields became hard pickers with a free-text fallback (ADR-0023 refinement). `ansible_serverlist_yml()` used to stamp the mission's values onto every VM, so `deploy_vms.vm_datacenter`/`vm_datastore` were editable but dead. New `ansible_effective_datacenter()`/`ansible_effective_datastore()` resolve per VM (own value wins, empty inherits the mission); the mission values stay mandatory at both readiness gates, so an empty override always resolves, and the `mission_configuration` block stays mission-wide. **Migration `0014_normalize_vm_location_overrides` (data-only, no DDL) is required for correctness:** the VM editor used to prefill both fields with the mission value and write it back, so existing rows hold copies rather than overrides — left alone they would have become silent, permanent overrides the moment the deploy started honouring them. The migration clears the exact copies (binary compare, so a deliberate case variant survives) and the editor now renders the mission value as a placeholder only. Mission and VM editors share a new `inventory_combo_field()` (`lib/inventory_field.php`) that pairs a `<select>` from the inventory cache with the real free-text `<input>`, replacing the loose `<datalist>`s; a stored value the inventory does not know renders as an extra selected option. With exactly one ESXi credential the list is exact, so a lone value is preselected in the form (replacing the desktop client's hardcoded `ha-datacenter`) and the per-VM datacenter control is hidden as pointless — while still posting a hidden input, because an unrendered field would come back as `''` and wipe the override. With several credentials the options are grouped per credential (`<optgroup>`) and nothing is preselected, since a mission does not store its target host. The RAM preset picker was generalized into the same `data-combo-input`/`data-combo-picker` pair in `app.js` (`syncComboPicker`/`syncComboInput`, `data-combo-clear` opt-in for the inherit semantics); init only syncs input→picker so a stored value can never be overwritten on load. **Known limit, now documented rather than silently rebuilt:** a per-disk datastore is not implementable with the current playbook — `vmware_guest`'s top-level `datastore` takes precedence over `disk[].datastore` and makes it be disregarded, and `createVMs-ESXi_playbook.yml` sets exactly that; by the same asymmetry a datastore override only takes effect when the VM is created (only the create playbook reads `datastore_name`, all four read `datacenter_name`). `esxi_inventory_mission_deviations()` was extended additively (new optional `vm_id`/`vm_name`) to cover the VM overrides it now has to police, plus interface VLANs, which also fixes the deviations table missing a stale NIC VLAN; the mission-list badge consequently lights up for VM-only deviations too. Two VLAN-reassignment bugs fixed on the integrations page: the destructive form was rendered whenever any active VLAN existed (now only when there is an actual `vlan` deviation to repair), and a "from" name matching nothing reported success with two zeros (now a warning flash). New `AnsibleLocationOverrideTest`, `VmLocationOverrideNormalizationTest`, and three cases in `EsxiVlanReassignTest`. New DE/EN keys across `mission_details`, `vm_edit` and `integrations`; new help section "Rechenzentrum und Datastore"; `docs/operations/esxi-inventory.md` and ADR-0023 updated.
- Settings page reorganized into four tabbed areas (`portal/settings.php`), presentation only, no wire or behaviour change, Playwright-verified in light and dark. The nine stacked panels are grouped and sorted into Bereitstellung (API base URL + read-only deploy runtime), Machine-API (IP allowlist, report-channel token, MECM reachability probe), Kataloge und Inventar (package-sync threshold, ESXi inventory interval) and System (timezone, backup status), reusing the existing CSP-safe `data-tabs`/`initTabs()` component (the help/logs pattern); without JS every panel stays visible. `initTabs()` gained a generic fallback: a hash pointing at an element *inside* a panel (the dashboard backup banner's `settings.php#panel-backup`) now opens the owning tab and scrolls to it, instead of landing on a hidden panel. The help "Einstellungen" tab (`lib/help/settings.php`) was reordered to mirror the four areas and gained the previously-missing ESXi inventory section; two now-stale structural phrases were corrected (the intro and the runtime card no longer say the runtime info sits "at the bottom of the page"/"the last section"). New DE/EN keys `settings.tabs_label`, `settings.tab_deploy|tab_machine_api|tab_catalog|tab_system` and `help.settings_esxi_heading|settings_esxi_p1`; lang-audit parity clean.
- Two-way lock between deploy mode and staggering on the queue form (`portal/deploy.php`, `app.js`), UX only; the server guard (`deploy_schedule_parse` rejecting a mode-incompatible stagger, `VIRTUSPHERE_DEPLOY_STAGGER_MODES`) and every wire/status semantic are unchanged. The mode→stagger direction already disabled+cleared the stagger field for the non-power modes; added the reverse: while a stagger interval > 0 is set, the non-staggerable options (Create VMs, Export MACs) are `disabled` in the mode dropdown and a hint under it explains why, so switching mode can no longer silently drop a typed interval. "Set" uses the server's semantics (`value !== '' && > 0`, so a typed `0` does not lock), and the currently-selected option is never disabled. New `deploy.stagger_lock_hint` DE/EN key. Browser-verified across the states (set/clear/zero, mode switch both ways).
- Portal spacing and row-action layout pass (ADR-0013), CSS/markup only, Playwright-verified in light and dark; no wire or behaviour change. (1) Checkboxes and radios keep their intrinsic size via a global `input[type=checkbox|radio]` rule in `base.css`; a bare toggle (the VM-list select-all and per-row bulk checkboxes) no longer inherits the text-field `width:100%`/`min-height` and rendered as a large box. (2) `.form-grid-full` sections (deploy verbose/VM-select/schedule, VM-editor hot-add) became a flex column with an even gap, and their individual controls are constrained: single toggle/radio rows size to their label, a standalone field caps at ~22rem, only a multi-column `.checkbox-grid` stretches; the two hot-add toggles moved into a `.checkbox-grid` so they sit compact side by side instead of two full-width bars. (3) A mid-panel filter button row directly above a table (the VLAN catalog status filter) gained the same breathing room a filter form has. (4) Row action buttons (`td.actions`) now lay out inline and compact, and the cell stays a real table-cell instead of `display:flex`: a flex `<td>` does not stretch to the row height, so its bottom border floated up at the button height and the row separator broke into detached stubs under the actions column (worst on the wide VM list with wrapped OS names); the user-admin cell keeps its stacked role/password controls via a new `.actions-stack`. ADR-0013 gains a "spacing and form layout" section documenting these as durable rules.
- Sortable portal list columns via a shared `lib/portal_sort.php` helper (loaded through `layout.php`). `portal_sort_apply()` whitelists the `?sort=`/`?dir=` GET keys and sorts server-side with a stable `usort` before render (ties keep the repository's default order); `portal_sort_header()` emits a CSP-safe clickable `.sort-link` header with an ▲/▼ glyph and `aria-sort` on the active column; `portal_sort_text()` sorts via `strnatcasecmp` (natural order, e.g. version strings), `portal_sort_number()` via `<=>`. Applied to the mission/template list (Name, VMs), the VM list (`portal/vms.php`: Name, Hostname, OS, CPU, RAM, Status) and the read-only package catalog (`portal/packages.php`: all six columns). Base query params ride along on the sort links (`type`, `mission_id`, the package `status` filter — whose filter form also carries the current sort in hidden fields so filtering keeps the order), and the mission/VM CSV export links carry the active `sort`/`dir` so the download matches the on-screen order. Invalid `sort`/`dir` fall back to the default column/ascending. New `common.sort_by` DE/EN key and a `.sort-link` style in `base.css` (inherits the muted header colour, underlines on hover/focus). No JS, no wire change; sorting is display-only (ADR-0014: never used for auth/RBAC/status/wire decisions). Playwright-verified across all three pages incl. edge cases.
- Small UI-polish fixes across the portal chrome, no wire or behaviour change. (1) Stacked form labels get breathing room between the caption and the control they wrap: a `label > input/select/textarea/.compound-field { margin-top: 6px }` rule in `components.css`, with `.filter-field`/`.checkbox-item` opting back out so their horizontally laid-out controls stay vertically centred; previously the bold caption sat almost on top of its field on every form (e.g. the settings MECM-host/TCP-port pair). (2) The user-admin role button now reads "Rolle ändern"/"Change role" instead of the ambiguous "Rolle"/"Role" (`lang/{de,en}/users.php`, `btn_role`). (3) The sidebar (`layout.css`) switches to `100dvh` and scrolls its own overflow (`overflow-y: auto` + `overscroll-behavior: contain`) so short/low desktop windows (wide but not tall, which never trip the width-only mobile breakpoint) no longer clip the admin nav group and the session timer below the fold; the scrollbar itself is kept slim and quiet (`scrollbar-width: thin` plus a subtle `::-webkit-scrollbar` pill) so it stays absent on tall windows and reads as a hint instead of the chunky default gutter when a short window does force a scroll. Playwright-verified across light/dark, tall/short/tiny and the mobile breakpoint.
- Disabled-button affordance and import-help completeness. A global `.button:disabled`/`.button[disabled]` rule in `components.css` (0.5 opacity, `cursor: not-allowed`, no hover sheen) makes a blocked action button read as inactive instead of only refusing the click, e.g. the mission-import confirm when a VLAN is missing or a VM name collides; the disabled state was previously styled for form inputs only (ADR-0013 baseline). The missions export/import help gains `help.transfer_p4` (DE/EN) covering the mission-name uniqueness/no-spaces rule and the 2 MB upload cap, the import edge cases that were surfaced only inline. Browser-verified; no wire or behaviour change.
- Login screen visual refresh (ADR-0013). The unauthenticated login page (`portal/login.php`) gets a scoped look-and-feel pass, confined to `.login-*` selectors in `components.css` so no shared `.panel`/`.button`/token rule and no other page is affected: a soft, slowly drifting aurora behind the card (reuses the `--bg-accent-glow` tokens, dialed down and `prefers-reduced-motion`-aware), an elevated glass card with a 2px accent hairline, a gradient wordmark, a glowing **real** logo (unchanged `logo-160.png`, halo via `drop-shadow`), icon-prefixed username/password inputs whose focus state swaps the global `:focus-visible` outline for an equivalent accent border + soft outward glow, and an accent-gradient submit button with a hover sheen and nudging arrow. New `login.tagline` DE/EN key ("Bereitstellung & Verwaltung" / "Provisioning & Management"). Also nonce-fixed the `app.js` `<script>` in `login.php` — the one portal head that included it without the CSP nonce, now matching `layout.php`. Playwright-verified in light and dark; ADR-0013 gains a "login is the one sanctioned exception to the restrained baseline" paragraph (incl. the login-only focus-ring substitution).
- ESXi inventory scheduling fixes found in a full-plan audit (ADR-0023). (1) `esxi_inventory_enqueue_due` now gates on `last_attempt_at` instead of only `last_success_at`: a credential that never succeeded (misconfigured host/rights, non-auth) was re-enqueued every ~5-minute check cycle instead of once per interval; both `record_success` and `record_failure` stamp `last_attempt_at`, so healthy credentials behave unchanged while a broken one retries at the interval. Auth failures still pause entirely. (2) Implemented the previously-missing E3.4b trigger: a successful `create`/`full` deploy (new VMs, changed datastore allocation) now enqueues an inventory refresh for its ESXi credential (`VIRTUSPHERE_DEPLOY_INVENTORY_REFRESH_MODES`, fail-soft after the job is finalized, double-enqueue guard prevents pile-up); power-cycle/start/export do not trigger one. New regression test in `EsxiInventoryEnqueueTest`; `docs/operations/esxi-inventory.md` sync-triggers updated.
- Inventory-deviation surfacing and guided VLAN reassignment (Paket E4/E4b refinement, ADR-0023). `esxi_inventory_mission_deviations()` flags any non-template mission whose datacenter, datastore or VLAN is absent from the current (non-empty) inventory of that kind — evaluated per kind so an empty/never-pulled inventory yields no false positives. It drives three warn-only surfaces: a deviations table on the integrations page, an "Inventar-Abweichung" badge on the mission list (`esxi_inventory_deviating_mission_ids()`), and an amber hint when a deviating mission is selected on the deploy page (`deploy.inventory_deviation_warn`). None of these block a deploy. The integrations page also gains a **guided VLAN mass reassignment** (`repo_reassign_vlan()`): one transaction rewrites every mission `wds_vlan` and interface `vlan` name-string from a free-text source to an active-catalog target, with one aggregated audit entry; portal records only, nothing on ESXi, gated by `missions.write`+`vms.write` with the form visibility matching that gate (each integrations POST action now carries its own gate instead of a blanket `deploy.run`). The VM editor's interface VLAN picker now mirrors the mission editor: active catalog entries only, with the stored value staying selectable as "… (nicht im aktuellen Inventar)" when retired/unknown. New integration test `EsxiVlanReassignTest` (reassignment counts + leaves non-matching rows, empty/identical-arg rejection, deviation detection incl. template exclusion). Test-hygiene fix: `EsxiVlanSyncTest` now snapshots and restores pre-existing (non-prefixed) catalog rows in setUp/tearDown so a full suite run no longer retires real dev VLANs as a side effect of the global sync it exercises.
- Restrained glassmorphism design pass (Paket D, ADR-0013). A single glass token set in `base.css` (`--glass-bg`, `--glass-border`, `--glass-blur`, `--glass-shadow` and a subtle `--bg-accent-glow` body gradient, per theme) applies translucent `backdrop-filter` surfaces to the chrome only: sidebar, topbar, panels/cards, alerts and the session modal. Data surfaces stay solid: tables (`.table-wrap`) and form controls keep an opaque background so contrast is unaffected. A `@supports not (backdrop-filter)` fallback swaps every glass surface back to opaque `--surface` (no grey haze); both themes stay WCAG-AA; no external assets, no inline styles, at most two stacked blur layers. Playwright-verified across pages in light and dark. ADR-0013 updated.
- ESXi-owned VLAN catalog + inventory-driven mission editor (Paket E chunk 4, ADR-0023). After every successful inventory pull `repo_esxi_vlan_sync` rebuilds `deploy_vlan` from the union of cached portgroups (across all credentials, fresh + frozen cache): a name present is upserted/un-retired, a name absent is **retired, never deleted**, and only when there is fresh positive evidence (a credential's last fetch succeeded) and the present set is non-empty (empty-guard) — a failed/stale fetch is never an absence proof, so an ESXi outage freezes the catalog on its last good state. `vlans.php` becomes a read-only catalog (ADR-0020 pattern): status filter (active/retired/all), per-portgroup host-presence badges, no create/edit, an admin cleanup-delete for retired rows only. The mission editor offers the active catalog for WDS-VLAN (the stored value stays selectable even if retired/unknown) and datacenter/datastore suggestion lists from the inventory cache (`<datalist>`, free-text fallback, no JS); assignments stay name-strings and are never rewritten. New sync integration test (upsert/un-retire/retire/no-fresh-fetch/empty-guard). Legacy `createVLAN`/`updateVLAN` stay for the token API until E3 (ADR-0023 known limit).
- ESXi inventory, read-only (Paket E, ADR-0023). Migration `0013_esxi_inventory` makes `deploy_jobs.mission_id` nullable (mission-less system jobs), adds `deploy_esxi_inventory` (`kind` ENUM mirrored from `VIRTUSPHERE_INVENTORY_KINDS`, checked by `check-enum-sync.sh`), `deploy_esxi_inventory_state` and `deploy_vlan.retired_at`. A new mission-less `inventory` deploy mode runs a read-only playbook (`inventoryESXi_playbook.yml`, only community.vmware `*_info`/`*_facts`) on a separate deploy-worker branch (the deploy path is unchanged), parses a base64-JSON marker and writes the cache via `lib/repo/esxi_inventory.php`: per-(credential,kind) atomic replace with an empty-result guard and case-insensitive de-dupe, plus fetch-state tracking that classifies errors (unreachable/auth/authz/ssh/parse) and pauses on auth to protect the ESXi account. The maintenance worker enqueues due credentials on an interval (`esxi_inventory_interval_hours`, default 6, 0=off); saving an ESXi credential triggers an immediate pull; a manual refresh (Gate `deploy.run`) lives on the integrations page. The integrations page shows, per credential, a traffic light, the fetch time + error category, a host RAM/CPU line (with a clock-skew note), datacenter/portgroup lists and a datastores table with thin capacity bars (colour/width set in `app.js` from a data attribute, CSP-clean). Settings gains an inventory-interval card. Never destructive: a failed fetch is never an absence proof, mission/VM fields are name-strings the sync never touches, and a credential deletion cascades only its cache rows. New parse/classification unit tests and cache/enqueue integration tests. Docs: ADR-0023 (+index), `docs/operations/esxi-inventory.md` (rights profiles, error categories, offline guarantee), help integrations panel. Verified against a productive host on rollout; requires a non-free ESXi license.
- CPU/RAM hot-add options for VM creation (Paket F). Migration `0012_vm_hotplug` adds `deploy_vms.cpu_hotplug`/`ram_hotplug` (`TINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 1`, mirrored in `struktur.sql`); existing and legacy-API-created rows get the default on. The VM editor gains two hot-add checkboxes (on by default, hidden+checkbox so unchecking submits 0); `repo_validate_vm_payload` normalizes them via `repo_vm_flag_value` and they join `REPO_VM_COLUMNS` so clone and JSON export/import carry them (field-tolerant: old exports default to on). `ansible_serverlist_yml` emits per-VM `hotadd_cpu`/`hotadd_memory`, and `createVMs-ESXi_playbook.yml` maps them into the `vmware_guest` `hardware` block (previously a hardcoded `hotadd_memory: true`, now `item.hotadd_cpu`/`item.hotadd_memory` with a `default(true)`). Flags apply only at VM creation; existing ESXi VMs are never reconfigured. Machine-API wire and the legacy `createVM` path are unchanged (DB default covers them). New YAML-generation unit test; `DEPLOYMENT.md` variable contract updated; help "CPU/RAM hot-add" section.
- Bulk VM-list actions (Paket C). `portal/vms.php` gains a checkbox column with a select-all (indeterminate state, live count in `app.js`) and a bulk action bar to delete selected VMs or reset their MECM ID in one go, gated by `vms.write` like the per-row actions. `repo_bulk_delete_vms`/`repo_bulk_reset_mecm_ids` (new, tested) reuse the existing per-VM guards: delete removes only portal records (never the hypervisor) and skips every VM while the mission has an active deploy job; reset reuses `repo_reset_vm_mecm_id` and skips VMs without an imported MAC. The result is an aggregated flash ("n done, m skipped (reasons)") and one aggregated audit entry per action; selections are capped at 200 (`VIRTUSPHERE_VM_BULK_CAP`). Reset stays blocked on templates. New integration test. Docs: help "several VMs at once" section, CHANGELOG.
- Portal timezone and deploy scheduling (Paket B, ADR-0022). **Timezone:** `db()` now pins every MySQL session to `SET time_zone = '+00:00'`, so storage/comparison stay in UTC regardless of the container clock; a new display-only setting `portal_timezone` (default `Europe/Berlin`) drives one formatter (`lib/portal_time.php` `portal_format_datetime()`), and the SSoT `portal_format_timestamp()` now delegates to it, so every existing timestamp render follows automatically. A settings "time" card shows the server time with offset and warns on browser-vs-server clock drift (>2 min) via a CSP-nonced JSON island read by `app.js`. **Note:** displayed existing times shift from UTC to local (Europe/Berlin +1h/+2h); nothing stored changes. **Scheduling:** migration `0011_deploy_job_scheduling` adds nullable `deploy_jobs.scheduled_at` (UTC DATETIME) + `group_id CHAR(12)` and indexes `(status, scheduled_at)`/`(group_id)`; the worker claim query gains `AND (scheduled_at IS NULL OR scheduled_at <= UTC_TIMESTAMP())`. A single Termin is one job; **staggering** creates one job per VM (`repo_enqueue_deploy_group`) sharing a `group_id` with `scheduled_at = base + i×N min` (power-on modes only), the single-active guard checked once for the group, ascending id = boot order. "Cancel group" stops only still-queued slots. `deploy_parse_schedule()` (unit-tested) interprets the datetime-local as portal wall time → UTC and rejects past (5-min grace), the 30-day horizon, a DST gap, or an out-of-range/mode-incompatible stagger; a preview/confirm step shows only when a schedule is set (immediate deploy stays one click). Missions with waiting scheduled jobs get a stronger delete confirmation (FK cascade). New unit + integration tests. Global `[hidden] { display:none !important }` CSS normalization (author `label`/grid display otherwise beats the UA rule). Docs: ADR-0022 (+index), help panels (settings time, deploy schedule).
- Raised the default System-disk size for new VMs from 40 GB to 50 GB. The value lives in the `VIRTUSPHERE_VM_DEFAULTS['disk_size_gb']` SSoT (`lib/defaults.php`); the VM-editor size input, the Ansible artifact fallback and the repo-layer defaults all derive from it, so only the constant changed. `DEPLOYMENT.md` updated to match.
- Added a minimal GitHub Actions CI baseline (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, Paket 0) that runs the same check-set documented in `docs/QA.md` on every push to `main` and every pull request: `php -l` over all first-party `Docker/WebAPI/**/*.php`, `composer run test:unit` (unit + static suites), `php scripts/lang-audit.php --ci`, `node --check` on `assets/app.js`, the three SSoT/doc-drift guards (`check-enum-sync.sh`, `check-php-version-sync.sh`, `check-doc-hygiene.sh`) and `lint-csp-patterns.sh` over the PHP files changed in the push/PR. It uses `shivammathur/setup-php` (PHP 8.4, `mysqli`+`sodium`) and Node 20 without a MySQL server, so the integration suite skips itself and only the unit + static suites run. PHPStan/Psalm and browser E2E stay out of this stage and are re-evaluated at E3. Documented in `docs/QA.md` (new "Continuous Integration" section) and noted in ADR-0015.
- Backup visibility and mission transfer (Paket A, ADR-0021). `scripts/backup.sh` now writes a JSONL status line per run (`backup-status.jsonl`, Unix-epoch `ts`, sizes, `keep`, free-disk %), guarded by `flock` and truncated to 90 lines; only the `status/` subdirectory is bind-mounted read-only into the `php` service (never the dumps/config tar with `.env`). A single reader (`lib/backup_status.php`, unit-tested, fail-soft) derives `ok|failed|stale|disk_low|unknown` (severity `failed>stale>disk_low`) and drives a new backup card on `settings.php`, an admin-only dashboard banner (rendered in `layout.php`, only when not `ok`) and an informative `backup` field on `health.php` (HTTP code unchanged). New `.alert-warning` utility. **Mission export/import** (`lib/mission_transfer.php`): a mission detail page exports its mission + VMs + interfaces (without MAC) + disks + package-by-name references as versioned JSON; the mission list imports it via a two-step dry-run preview (server-side session hand-off, 10-min TTL) that reports counts, resolved/missing packages, missing VLANs and global VM-name conflicts, then writes in one transaction reusing the portal validators (VMs come in fresh, MACs never carried). Missing VLANs and global VM-name collisions block the import; missing packages are skipped. **CSV list export** (`lib/portal_export.php`) on the mission and VM lists: semicolon delimiter, UTF-8 BOM, formula-injection guard. New round-trip integration test and reader unit test. Docs: ADR-0021 + index, `docs/operations/backup.md` (status channel, coverage table, troubleshooting), help panels (settings backup card, stack day-to-day + backup troubleshooting, missions export/import), `DEPLOYMENT.md`/`INSTALLATION-ANLEITUNG.md` (status mount, backup-before-migration rule). **Refactor:** `portal/help.php` (409 lines, over the 400-line soft limit and about to grow with Paket E) was split into per-panel partials under `lib/help/` (not web-reachable, nginx denies `/lib/`); `help.php` is now a 54-line tab shell (ADR-0006).

## 2026-07-08

- Made the OS catalog page (`portal/os.php`) read-only for create/edit, matching `packages.php`, while keeping an admin-only delete. `deploy_os` is owned by the MECM packages sync (`mecm_Packages-TaskSeq-sync.ps1` → `mecm_packages.php`), which upserts Task Sequences and retires missing ones; create/edit were redundant and drifted (a hand-typed name that matches no Task Sequence is retired on the next sync, an edit is overwritten on the next upsert). The page now renders read-only rows with a status filter (Active/Retired/All) and a per-OS VM-usage count, plus an admin `catalog.write` delete kept as a safe self-healing cleanup: if the Task Sequence still exists in MECM the next sync re-creates the row, deployment/PXE uses the VM's stored `vm_os` (not this catalog), and a referenced OS stays purge-protected. The delete confirmation shows how many VMs use the OS (new `repo_os_vm_counts`). `createOS`/`updateOS`/`deleteOS` repo functions stay for the legacy token API (`access.php`) until E3; `os.php` is listed under `catalog.write` in `PermissionParityTest` again because delete makes it a write handler. Docs updated: ADR-0020, the help catalog (new OS section in the Packages tab, `perm_catalog_write`, stack/integration texts) and `mecm-integration.md`.
- Stack-weiter Sicherheits- und SSoT-Härtungslauf (Audit über PHP, PowerShell, Docs). **Legacy-Token-API RBAC (H1):** `access.php` gated mutierende Aktionen (`createOS`/`updateOS`/`deleteOS`, `create/update/deleteVLAN`, `create/update/deleteMission`, VM-Writes) jetzt über die Rolle des Token-Inhabers mit denselben Permissions wie das Portal (`role_has_permission`). Neue Spalte `deploy_tokens.user_id` (Migration `0010_legacy_token_user`, in `struktur.sql` mit FK gespiegelt); `generateToken()` bindet den Token an den ausstellenden Benutzer, `legacyTokenRole()` löst die Rolle auf (unbekannte/alte Tokens fallen auf die niedrigste Rolle). Read-Actions und Wire-Contract der MECM-/Report-Endpoints bleiben unverändert (E3-Regel: additive Härtung, kein Endpoint-Removal). **Weitere Fixes:** `vms.php` gated den `reset_mecm_id`-POST jetzt über `can('vms.write')` (vorher ungeschützt), Reset-Button in `vm_edit.php` an `$canWrite` gebunden, und `PermissionParityTest` prüft nun, dass der Write-Guard vor der ersten `$action`-Verzweigung steht (M1); `db_importMAC.php` liefert im Fehlerfall die generische `Interner Serverfehler`-Meldung statt der rohen Exception und ist im `PhaseCContractTest`-Invariant erfasst (M2); die generischen `Throwable`-Catches in `credentials/os/vlans/missions/mission_details/vm_edit` nutzen `portal_error_message()` statt `getMessage()` (M3); `api/login.php` übergibt Zugangsdaten roh an `generateToken()` (kein `htmlspecialchars` mehr, das Passwörter mit `& < > " '` zerstörte, L1). **SSoT/Drift:** Retire-Schwellwert-Grenzen als Konstanten (`VIRTUSPHERE_PACKAGE_RETIRE_THRESHOLD_MIN/MAX`), DHCP-Modus über `data-mode-select` statt JS-Literal, `vm_status`-Default-Kommentar auf `constants.php` als SSoT, Portal-Beispiel-Hostname `virtusphere.local` → `virtusphere.lan`, korrigierter Rollen-ENUM-Kommentar in `permissions.php`, Em-Dashes aus `go-live.md` entfernt. **PowerShell:** Installer härtet die Registry-ACL des Config-Keys auf `SYSTEM`/`Administratoren` und liest den `ReportToken` verdeckt statt als Klartext-Argument (M4/M5); Warnung bei beschreibbarem Paket-`files`-Ordner (H2); Autoimporter setzt `-ErrorAction Stop` am Deployment-Type und rollt bei Fehler die unvollständige Application zurück (M6); `version` mit Bindestrich wird abgelehnt (M7); `Package_Vorlage/config.json` an die Doku angeglichen (`DeployTo` leer, `ErrorAction` dokumentiert, M8). Neue Integrationstests `AccessLegacyRbacTest` (RBAC-Gate + Sonderzeichen-Login) und `MissionsRepoTest` (Rename-Lock, Clone-Preflight, requireLocation). Behobener `set -u`-Bug in `scripts/lint-csp-patterns.sh` (arithmetische Expansion `$[A-Za-z_]` in den einfach-Quote-SQL-Mustern). Docs `mecm-integration.md` (vollständige Registry-Wertliste, Token-/Share-Absicherung) aktualisiert.
- User admin page (`portal/users.php`) UX fixes. Validation failures no longer show the same field message twice: the flash is now the generic `users.flash_check_input` ("Bitte Eingaben prüfen." / "Please check your input.") while the specific error stays inline, and `.form-grid` switched from `align-items: end` to `start` so an inline `.field-error` grows downward instead of pushing its own input out of line with the neighbours. The "Zuletzt gesehen"/"Last Seen" column was renamed to "Zuletzt angemeldet"/"Last Login" since `last_seen_at` is only written at login, not on activity. The stacked action controls (`td.actions` in `components.css`) are now compact: the column sizes to content (`width: 1%`) with fixed 130px role select / password inputs and their buttons inline, instead of full-width controls inflating the column. The almost-always-empty "Gesperrt bis"/"Locked until" column was dropped; an active login lock now surfaces as a "Gesperrt"/"Locked" badge in the "Aktiv" column (with an auto-until tooltip) instead of a dedicated column of raw timestamps. Added a "Sperre aufheben"/"Clear lock" action (`clear_lock`, audited) that nulls `locked_until`, shown only while a login lock is active; help text `usersmgmt_safety_3` updated to match.
- Added a per-page deep-link into the help system and an admin-only "Settings" help tab. `layout_header()` gained an optional `$helpAnchor`; when set it renders a "? Hilfe/Help" pill next to the page title (`.page-title-row`/`.page-help-link` in `layout.css`, new `layout.help_page_link`/`help_page_title` DE/EN keys) linking to `help.php#panel-<anchor>`, which the existing `initTabs()` hash logic opens. Wired on the five pages that have a matching tab: missions/templates → missions, deploy, packages, integrations, settings. New `settings` tab in `portal/help.php` (gated by `can('system.config')`, inserted before "Architecture") documents all six settings sections for rotating admins — API base URL, machine-API IP allowlist (deny-by-default, clients auth via MAC), report-channel token (registry `ReportToken`), package-sync protection threshold, MECM reachability probe (empty host = last device-sync IP, port 445) and the read-only runtime info — fact-checked against `machine_api.php`, `maintenance_worker.php`, `mecm_packages.php` and `settings.php`. Corrected two stale claims in the Overview role card (`help.roles_admin_item3` no longer names a non-existent HTTPS-config setting; `item6` now lists the probe target + package-sync threshold instead of non-existent "reachability thresholds") and fixed two DE `settings` strings that read "API base URL" in the German catalog (`api_base_url_label`, `api_base_url_missing` → "API-Basis-URL"). The German help already uses "API-Basis-URL", so no help change was needed for the label.
- Split the audit log viewer (`portal/logs.php`) into three server-rendered tabs so the protocol is no longer one flat table: "Access & system" (system, legacy API, users, credentials, settings), "Resources" (missions, OS, VMs, VLANs) and "Deployment" (deploy, MECM). Tabs are `<a>` links carrying `?tab=` (no JS, each tab keeps its own server-side pagination); the grouping lives in the new `VIRTUSPHERE_LOG_TABS` constant, `repo_log_filter()`/`repo_count_logs()`/`repo_recent_logs()` now take a category list and build a prepared `category IN (...)`, and the category dropdown is scoped to the active tab. Reuses the existing `.tab` styling via a new `a.tab`/`aria-current` variant. Help page (Integrations & Logs) and `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` updated to describe the sections.
- Restructured the help page (`portal/help.php`) into five category tabs (Overview, Missions & VMs, Deployment, Packages, Integrations & Logs) and rewrote the Missions & VMs and Integrations & Logs tabs as plain-language operator guidance for rotating admin staff (sourced from `docs/operations/mecm-integration.md`): mission↔SCCM-collection relationship, naming/data-quality rules with their reasons, per-stage VM status explanations with "when stuck" checklists, the two-step VM decommission procedure, the five monitored integration sources with traffic-light semantics and typical diagnoses, the four client phases incl. "confirmation pending", and log kinds/categories/retention; the admin role list now mentions the MECM integration settings. Added a sixth "Architecture" tab: a ten-question FAQ (native `<details>` accordion, `.faq` styles) explaining the whole stack — components, data ownership, deploy-job flow (worker → SFTP/SSH → Ansible → ESXi), MAC export, device sync, PXE/OS install, package pipeline, connection directions, the machine API and failure behavior — fact-checked against compose file, worker/SSH code, sync scripts and `mecm_report.php`. New CSP-safe tab component (`data-tabs` markup, `.tab-list`/`.tab` styles, `initTabs()` in `assets/app.js`) with ARIA tablist semantics, arrow-key navigation and `#panel-*` hash deep-links; without JS the tab bar stays hidden and all sections remain visible.
- MECM server-script robustness pass (`Powershell-MECM/mecm/`): missing registry config now self-heals in a 60s wait loop instead of `exit 1` (task-scheduler restarts were exhausted after ~3 minutes); the device-sync import-race check re-probes device existence instead of matching the locale/version-dependent "already exists" error text, caches `ResourceID` per scan (drops up to three `Get-CMDevice` single queries per device), and warns on multiple DHCP NICs; the autoimporter cleanup also finds old versions without their own collection, retries incomplete removals and missing deployments idempotently (file-stamp is only persisted on clean scans), and previously silent `catch {}` paths now log. `VirtuSphere_Applications` folder name moved to a shared SSoT in `VirtuSphere-Common.ps1`. Edge-case reference tables added to `docs/operations/mecm-integration.md`.
- MECM integration hardening across the machine API, portal and PowerShell scripts (ADR-0018, plan stages E0–E6). New display-only report channel `Docker/WebAPI/mecm_report.php` (POST `reportPhase`/`heartbeat`, optional SHA-256-hashed `X-VirtuSphere-Token`, IP/MAC auth, dedupe/size/rate guards) backed by new tables `deploy_client_events` (VM-cascade, 30-day retention) and `deploy_integration_heartbeats` (migration `0007`, mirrored in `struktur.sql`).
